# LEDGER: decisions

One entry per material choice: date, decision, reason, who, link. Canon
outranks this file. Everything decided before 24 September, and still
binding, is in production/archive/DECISIONS-to-2026-09-24.md with its records.

- 2026-09-28 | The week's way of working: one goal set with /goal runs to Sunday evening; our stop hook is removed (settings, its script and tools/sitting-clock.py) so the two cannot disagree; sittings end, and a dated summary a day replaces the summary per sitting, with the day's page first and free space on C: before and after; cleanup and backup at the end of each day. Research first before new kinds of work, after two failed attempts, before relying on memory about tools, versions, APIs or licences, and before declaring anything impossible; by a separate helper, about thirty minutes, dated sources, saved under production/research/. A second session, the town session, in the worktree ledger-town on the branch town, for work that needs no graphics card; handovers as single lines in NOW.md; both fetch and rebase before every push. - 2026-09-28 | Knowing a little shows. A story of Tom's nights held below the share floor (the mill keeps it about eleven more days as it fades) puts its holder on a Notices floor. The look follows the gaze research: a stranger glances from about 10 m for half a second and looks away by 2.4 m; one who notices him looks again in the passing zone and holds it 1.5 s; one who watches keeps him in view and looks back. Some say a half-remembered word about him to a companion, once per story (a fixed draw against the story's share of the floor). In talk, a cooler, vague manner in place of "at ease". Only people who can tell it is him (Acquaintance.CanNameYou) show anything. All in Core (StreetVoice.RegardFor, ConversationEngine.HeardManner); no mill constant changed. On Quay Street's ten, a hearer's knowing shows about 110 street-hours instead of 11 to 31, and the busiest hour grows by at most one person. Two independent checks. | Jafar's town list, item 1. | Claude (his to overrule) | production/research/gaze-and-knowing/SUMMARY-2026-09-28.md
- 2026-09-28 | Friends meet. Every named person with a friendship has a routine written pair by pair (production/specs/hook-cast.json: 40 people, the prototype's 80 live friendships unchanged, by weekday, read by Core's CastDay): all 80 now meet, where 55 never did. How often is an authoring rule, as no measured figures were found: a friendship of 0.6 or more meets on at least five days a week, 0.45 to 0.55 on three, weaker ones on one. Places and rhythms from the research: the fish dock from six, the market on Tuesday, Friday and Saturday, Wednesday early closing, pensions on Thursday (an assumption). No constant changed. News reach, every person as witness, at 30 minutes of play (the prototype city's 23 September figures, then now): half sure 0.52 to 0.63 people told, witnesses reaching nobody 73% to 69%; 0.6 sure 0.84 to 1.50, 65% to 39%; sure 1.58 to 3.93, 60% to 4%. At half sure the retelling arithmetic, not the routines, is what stops it: a half-sure story cannot pass along a friendship under 0.5, and half the cast's friendships are 0.3 to 0.4. | Jafar's town list, item 2. | Claude (his to overrule) | production/research/small-town-meetings/SUMMARY-2026-09-28.md
- 2026-09-28 | Live talk is checked detail by detail. The checker lists every specific a line states, each with the numbered item the character knows that supports it; the Core, not the model, fails any specific with no valid source, and any event sourced only to the card, the scene or the time now; each flagged detail then gets its own second look, which must name a real item and is not shown the line. The first sentence is still spoken as soon as it passes; the fallback line has six wordings. | On 240 held-back test turns, invented details in what the player hears fell from 30% to 5%; the check catches 90% of invented replies (33% before) and flags 30% of honest ones (3%); the fallback ends 7% of turns, as before. The cost: when the first sentence fails (14% of turns) the player waits for the whole turn, up to about 8 s (town list 6a). Three rounds of the independent check; its known limits are in FINDINGS. | Claude (town list item 3) | production/research/invented-claims/RESULTS-2026-09-28.md
- 2026-09-28 | Act III reads as a cab office. The letter is a VAT inspection under Schedule 7 to the Value Added Tax Act 1983 asking for the VAT account, the books, the booking record and the drivers' sheets; the inspector is an officer of Customs and Excise from the VAT office; the cellar is the back room; the endings say "cab office". The laundering ceiling stays a third of honest takings (the field is now TakingsToDate), reasoned from what a VAT officer compared (account invoices against bookings and drivers' sheets, takings against cars, drivers and costs): the research found the checks but no source for where they bite, and a new number would move the endings' balance. The rest of the old Unity world's pub is left with that layer (FINDINGS). | D58's wording order (queue 400); production/research/cab-office-1990. | Claude, under D58 | ledger/Assets/Scripts/Core/ActThree.cs

- 2026-09-28 | Every live model call can go through a relay of ours (ledger/Relay, ASP.NET Core: MIT, part of the .NET SDK already used). The game holds a copy's code, never a key; the relay chooses the model for each role, caps each reply, takes only the game's own four kinds of request (a character's line, the claim check, its second look, a night's reflection), counts each copy's allowance in dollars from real usage (by default $0.50 a day and $5 a month) and stops every call at 80% of our month's budget; it logs sizes and costs, never words. The helper uses it with --relay and --copy. Where it runs, and the budget, are Jafar's. | ROADMAP's thirty-minute build for his friends; the runtime-AI research: the key must not ship, a server that answers anyone is only a leak moved, and the provider's cap stops every call until the 1st. Self-test 21 of 21 against a stand-in provider. | Claude (town list 6b); hosting and budget his | ledger/Relay
- 2026-09-28 | Players are told the town talks through an AI model before their first conversation, and can report any line. The notice's words live in the Core (AiNotice) and reach the game in the talk helper's ready message; a report ({"report": id, "why": note}) keeps that turn (the player's line, the reply, the note) and goes to our relay, which keeps it for us, or is kept on the PC when there is none or it cannot be reached; every reply says whether the model wrote it. What shows on screen is handed to the builder. | Steam, Microsoft and PEGI want a way to report live AI output; the EU's AI Act (Article 50, since 2 August 2026) wants people told at their first interaction and generated text marked in a form a machine can read. | Claude (town list 6c) | ledger/Assets/Scripts/Core/AiNotice.cs
- 2026-09-28 | The content rule on every line said live, and a safety rule beside it. The engine now keeps what the player was shown (ResponseValidator runs before the reply is stored, so a refused line is remembered as the character changing the subject, and a heard first sentence alone when the rest is refused), checks each early first sentence itself, and refuses a reply that opens as a stage direction; the director's line and the nightly beliefs pass the same rules. A new SafetyRule, apart from canon's content gate, refuses any line urging the player to harm or kill themselves, narrow enough that "you'll kill yourself lifting that crate" and "you take your life in your hands crossing Quay Street" stand. | ROADMAP's "the content rule enforced on everything said live"; the open finding of 26 September; Steam's guardrail question. Five passes of the independent check; its British idioms are in the tests. | Claude (town list 6e) | ledger/Assets/Scripts/Core/SafetyRule.cs

- 2026-10-03 | LICENCES: nothing marked NoAI ('Allows usage with AI: No' on Fab) is used, in the game or the project, nor as a reference or input for making anything else (that is itself using it with AI). Every Fab item is checked before use and every asset's source and licence recorded. Replacements come from sources without restrictions (Poly Haven, ambientCG, CC0 scans on Sketchfab with his token); where nothing free fits, we make the object in Blender from real-world photographs and generated textures, judged by the gate like anything else. FOUND (3 October, each listing's own data read from this PC, isAiForbidden): all 35 items on his Fab list are NoAI (18 Megascans surfaces and decals, Epic's MetaHuman Clothing Construction Presets, Epic's Game Animation Sample, 15 Megascans goods); none was ever downloaded. In the game: only Epic's seven MetaHuman garment packs, all NoAI, removed. The shop windows' produce, bakes and rope are Poly Haven models (CC0) and our own Blender code, never Fab. The MetaHuman framework came with Unreal Engine 5.8 (its own plugins, not a Fab item); the Unreal Engine licence governs it and forbids using the engine as training input to generative AI or as prompt input where the AI trains on its input, and MetaHumans to build AI datasets or train or test AI, none of which we do provided his Claude account does not train on what we send. Fab's own NoAI clause (its licence, section 16(l)) is narrower than his ruling: datasets for, development of, or training inputs to generative AI. | His message of 3 October, urgent. | Jafar | NOW.md item 1b; production/specs/fab-free-megascans.md

## 13 October 2026, Rita's shop: dark goods at night stay dim (taste call), recorded
- Decision: Rita's shop is "ready for review" with one open row, LIT-08 (the black goods, such as the boombox, binoculars and camera, stay under 0.6 of the room's brightness at night). It cannot be met together with the burnt-pixel limit (PIC-05g): stronger strip lights burn the white goods and the red velvet first. Kept as the reviews worded it; dark goods in a dim window is the recommended answer.
- Reason: both limits come from his own look rules (no burnt highlights; goods lit as in a shop). The builder tested 6.7 times the light and the boombox stayed at 0.19 while 4.7 to 5.3 per cent of the frame burnt.
- Marked for Jafar: accept dark goods in a dim window (recommended), or lighter materials for the black goods (a change of the goods, not the light). Link: production/cloud-week/kit/shop-windows/NOTES.md, checks/check_ritas_v3.json.
